# PLANNED / BACKLOG — every unbuilt-but-intended thing, in one place

> **Companion to `CURRENT-STATE.md`.** That file says what IS; this one says what is **intended and
> not yet done**. Assembled 2026-08-26 (Prompt 141) by sweeping every design brief, prompt
> "follow-ups" section, gap-audit tail, status ledger and archived STATUS block in the repo.
>
> **Nothing here is a new idea.** Every row cites **where it came from** so you can read the original
> reasoning, and so a future consolidation can prove nothing was invented or dropped. Rows are kept
> even when they look dead — *a contemplated feature is never deleted, only re-ranked or explicitly
> retired with a reason.*
>
> **Before building any row: apply the P131 lens.** Classify the gap first —
> **(a)** the answer is already on-box and STRUCTURED → deterministic plumbing, **not** an LLM;
> **(b)** on-box but UNSTRUCTURED text → an LLM fits; **(c)** not on-box at all → neither, an LLM
> would fabricate. Two of the top-ranked local-model gaps turned out to be (a) and (c) once measured.
>
> **And re-measure the row before you build it.** Several rows below carry dates from July; the
> standing doctrine is that a dated blocker is a hypothesis, not a fact.

**Legend** — 🔴 not started · 🟡 partially built · 🟢 designed/specced, ready to build ·
⏳ built, pending a manual apply/flip · 👤 needs Scott (tenant / human decision) · ⚪ optional/roadmap ·
🔍 needs re-measure before it can be ranked honestly.

---

## P0b — ⭐ BD PIPELINE FUNNEL (re-audit 2026-08-28, Scott's framing: property → owner → chain → developer)

Source: `docs/audits/BD_PIPELINE_FUNNEL_AUDIT_2026-08-28.md`. **Every prior audit measured queued
work; this one measures the funnel.** gov: 13,835 live properties → 6,362 with an LCC owner link →
**149 with 2+ historical owner links (1.1%)**. Against the goal — *connected ownership history back
to the developer across target markets* — **that 1.1% is the number that matters.**

| ⭐ **B6d** | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-29 — and the two alerts everyone assumed were mis-sized SLAs are REAL BREAKS** ([`docs/audits/B6d_FEED_EXPECTATION_GRADING_2026-08-29.md`](../audits/B6d_FEED_EXPECTATION_GRADING_2026-08-29.md)). Fixed with **B6a's own lesson one layer up: an unwatched feed EMITS**, with a NULL bound as a *positive* statement, and a resolve arm keyed on that — **never on ABSENCE**, which also covers a feed whose query errored or whose mirror went blind. The residual is **counted as `alerts_orphaned`, never auto-resolved**: a decision and a disappearance must not close identically. **Retire the BOUND, never the ROW.** ⚠️ **THE POPULATION IS 25, NOT 23** — LCC Opps carries its own registry (`om_intake`, `salesforce_sync`), invisible to a count taken from the domain DBs. ⚠️ **RULE 3c HELD TWICE.** dia `medicare_clinics` (65d/45d): p50 gap 2d, **max gap ever 41d**, so 45 was never the problem — `ingestion_tracker` shows **27 failed + 6 abandoned** CMS runs since the last success 2026-06-25 while `dataset_modified_date` reads **2026-08-25**; widening would have buried a two-month outage against a LIVE source (**B6d-cms**). gov `sam_lease_opportunities` re-scoped 14 → 21 **and deliberately left violated at 33d**: the weekly producer is fine (`usajobs`, same workflow, landed 2026-08-24) and the SAM call itself returns **401 Unauthorized** (**B6d-sam**). ⚠️ **That 401 is NOT the §18 rate limit** — different key, different endpoint; §18's *"the 401 is not real"* correction is exactly what makes a real 401 easy to dismiss. ⚠️ **MEASURING A FEED'S OWN GAPS IS CIRCULAR WHEN IT HAS BEEN DEAD**: an outage is a CLOSED gap and enters its own distribution — `gsa_lease_change_facts` has 2 dates and one 170d gap (*the outage B6b repaired*), so 3×p90 derives a **510-day** bound; and a lifetime window mixes ERAS (`usajobs` p90 31.8/max 95 over life, p50 7/max 16 in the scheduled era). **B6a's p90 rule does not transfer from steps to feeds.** Primary basis = the producer's DECLARED schedule; below 3 gaps the honest verdict is `cannot_be_sized_from_data`, recorded not dressed up. **Weekly family → 21 = 3× cadence** (6 feeds at 14/30/35/45; 14 had already produced a false fire). ⚠️ **The GSA family is FOUR feeds, ONE publisher, and carried FOUR bounds** (65/35/45/45), three below the cycle's own peak: publication is monthly with a **21–51d lag**, peak data age ~82d → `gsa_leases_snapshot` **65 → 90** (it was **6 days from firing** on a healthy feed, with `consecutive_unchanged=3` proving GSA has not published August), derived trio **→ 75**, pinned by a guard so they cannot drift apart again. `gsa_lease_events` was 35 and **would have fired 2026-09-10**: its cadence changed 2026-08-10 when the fingerprint dedupe began skipping, and its bound had not. **`gsa_source_pull` stays tight at 21** — *did WE stop pulling* is a different question from *is GSA publishing*. **`opm_workforce` 120 → 200**: data is 74–75d old **at the moment of a successful import** and the one observed import interval is 119d, so 120 was **unmeetable by the process that feeds it** — it fired 3× in 3 months, every one closed "expected". Guards: LCC 7 tests **12/12 mutations RED**, gov 8 tests **14/14 RED**. ⚠️ **A REVIEW BOT CAUGHT A SECURITY CLAIM THIS ROUND ASSERTED WITHOUT POSITIVE-CONTROLLING IT** (Codex, on Dialysis#7378): `REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM anon, authenticated` on `compute_feed_cadence` was a **no-op** — Postgres grants EXECUTE on a new FUNCTION to **PUBLIC** by default, so both roles still reached the SECURITY DEFINER function; measured live after the "fix", `proacl` carried the leading `=X/postgres` PUBLIC grant and `has_function_privilege('anon', oid, 'EXECUTE')` read TRUE on gov and dia. Fixed with `REVOKE … FROM PUBLIC`, verified false after. A **VIEW** takes no default PUBLIC grant, which is why the view half of the same migration worked. **Assert a privilege with `has_function_privilege()`, never by reading the REVOKE you just wrote** — four artifacts repeated the unverified claim. `compute_feed_freshness` keeps its anon grant BY DESIGN and the guard pins that asymmetry. | ✅ | `prompts/B6d-*.md` · I4 · I11 |
| ⭐ **B6d-cms** | ✅ **ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED IN CODE 2026-08-29** ([`docs/audits/B6d_cms_INGESTION_REPAIR_2026-08-29.md`](../audits/B6d_cms_INGESTION_REPAIR_2026-08-29.md)); **the restart itself ships on the next Railway run — see the 👤 line below.** ⚠️ **THREE PREMISES IN THE BRIEF WERE WRONG AND EACH ONE MATTERED.** **(1) `cms-ingestion-daily.yml` DOES NOT EXIST** — deleted 2026-07-29 (`5d54fd7`, W1.5b); ingestion is the **Railway `cms-ingestion` cron** (`0 6 * * *`), so the GH Actions logs the brief pointed at hold nothing. **(2) "40 failed + 16 abandoned, still trying daily" is not 56 failed ingestions** — those totals **lump `source='ingestion_lock'` janitor rows in with pipeline rows** (27+6 are `cms_ingestion`; 13+10 are the lock), and the runs are **not daily**: **7 attempt-days in 100**, spaced **31 days** apart. That regularity was the answer, visible before any code was read. **(3) No failure carries a CMS error at all** — every `error_log` is a janitor artifact (*"Orphaned run… stuck in 'started' for 48.0h"*, *"Reclaimed by ingestion_lock after 0.0h stale"*). `run_cms_ingestion` records a real traceback on an exception, so **the ABSENCE of one is the evidence: the process is KILLED, not failing.** **The mechanism is two coupled defects, and neither alone explains it.** `main()` gated on **`if days_ago >= 30`** — a **calendar throttle capping a DAILY cron at ~one ingest/month** against a CMS republish cadence of **p50 2 days**; and `get_last_ingestion_meta()` took the newest tracker row **of ANY status**, so a run killed mid-flight recorded `dataset_modified_date` = CMS's new publish date and **suppressed its own retry** via `SKIP-GATE [last_run_date]`. **A latch: gate opens ~every 31d → run starts → is killed → the crash re-arms both gates.** ⚠️ **Removing the throttle alone would have fixed NOTHING** — the pipeline would run daily and then skip on a watermark written by a crashed run. Watermark now `("success",)` only; `partial` (didn't finish) and `recorded` (marks a *skip*) excluded too — **this check must fail OPEN.** ⚠️ **THE MEASUREMENT THAT REFRAMED IT: find what the job writes on EVERY run.** `cms_dataset_updates` shows **99 of the last 100 days, including 06:02 the morning of the audit** — so the cron is healthy and **CMS egress works**. A max answers *"did anything arrive"*, never *"was the feed ingested"*. FROM anon, authenticated` was a no-op because **PUBLIC** held the grant. Here `REVOKE ALL ... FROM public` is a no-op because **anon and authenticated hold EXPLICIT grants** (`proacl` reads `anon=X/postgres`, and the bare `=X` PUBLIC entry is genuinely gone) — almost certainly from Supabase's `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` at original CREATE time, which a later `CREATE OR REPLACE` preserves. `lcc_flush_provenance_events` is a live example: its migration ships `REVOKE ALL ... FROM public; GRANT EXECUTE ... TO service_role;`, which **reads as "service_role only" and is not** — `has_function_privilege('anon', ..., 'EXECUTE')` is **true**, and it is a definer writer into the append-only `field_provenance` ledger. **Assert a privilege with `has_function_privilege()`, never by reading the REVOKE you just wrote.** ⚠️ **DO NOT blanket-revoke.** `compute_feed_freshness` keeps an explicit `anon` grant BY DESIGN (the LCC cross-DB pull reads `v_feed_freshness` as anon) and revoking it would silently blind the freshness monitor; and it is unconfirmed from the sandbox whether `OPS_SUPABASE_KEY` is the service or anon key, so revoking on the flush could stop the live drain. **Triage per function: establish the caller's role first, then narrow.** Not touched by PR8 — pre-existing, unchanged in shape by it. | 🔴 | measured 2026-09-02 during PR8 |

| ✅ **PR12** | **SHIPPED 2026-09-02 — and the fix needed NO table rewrite.** `value_text_hash` was `GENERATED AS encode(sha224(value::text::bytea),'hex')`; jsonb renders backslash escapes and bytea's escape parser accepts only `\\` and `\ooo`, so the cast raised **22P02 and aborted the whole `lcc_merge_field()` call**. ⚠️ **THE SCOPE IS BROADER THAN THE DOUBLE QUOTE THIS ROW NAMED**: `"`, newline, tab, CR, backspace, formfeed and any control char break it, *including inside a jsonb object's or array's string members*. It does NOT break on a jsonb object's own delimiter quotes (`{"a": "b"}` carries no backslash) or on non-ASCII. Rule, validated 14/14 against the live cast: after collapsing `\\` pairs, ANY remaining backslash errors. ⚠️ **AND THE SIZING PREMISE IN THIS ROW WAS WRONG — `ALTER COLUMN ... DROP EXPRESSION` IS METADATA-ONLY.** Probed live on a scratch table in this database: `pg_relation_filenode` **2831316 before and after**, every value byte-identical. So the generated column became a plain column owned by a BEFORE trigger using `convert_to(...,'UTF8')` — **sub-second lock, no rewrite of 1,270,785 rows / 1,025 MB, no transient disk on the database whose documented worst failure is disk-full → sign-in lockout** (free disk is not measurable from SQL or the MCP surface, which is exactly why this mattered). No backfill: **0 of 1,270,785 stored values contain a backslash**, so the new expression reproduces every hash byte-for-byte — verified after apply at **0 mismatches over the whole population**, positive control 1,270,785. Controls: pre-fix quoted → `22P02` / plain → `NO_ERROR`; post-fix quoted, newline and jsonb-object-with-quoted-member → all `NO_ERROR`, 0 residue. **Exposure: 79 ladder-governed values, of which 12 are proven SAFE and 67 remain** (see PR12a). JS half: `shouldWriteField` still fails open — losing a curated value is worse than losing its provenance — but now records the DB's own SQLSTATE, counts it (`provenance_failed`), and opens a deduped `lcc_health_alerts(provenance_write_failed)`. Migration `20261010120000`; guard `test/pr12-provenance-hash-and-failure-signal.test.mjs` (12 tests, **17/17 mutations RED**). | ✅ | `docs/audits/PR12_PROVENANCE_QUOTE_LOSS_2026-09-02.md` |

Morice, SIOR`, `107 "H" St E` — plus JSON-array-in-a-text-column (BR1) and HTML garbage in `properties.address`. 🚨 **AND 67 IS NOT THE EXPOSURE — IT IS THE LADDER-GOVERNED HALF, WHICH IS ~6% OF IT.** Found by the post-fix check, not the census: **8 break-class rows landed within two hours of the migration**, live `costar_sidebar` writes of `dia.sales_transactions.notes` / `sale_notes_raw` — multi-line OM narrative, all lost the day before. | **PR12b** | 🟡 **`lcc_flush_provenance_events` advances its watermark PAST an errored event.** `v_max_id` is assigned before the per-event `EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS` block, so an event that fails still moves `max_event_id`, and any caller that persists that value as its cursor skips the event **permanently** — the failure is returned in `errors[]` but the event is gone. PR12 removed the only error that fired today; the shape survives for the next one. Fix: advance the watermark only on the last SUCCESSFUL event, or return the failed ids so the caller can retry. Found in PR12 §5, not changed there. | 🟡 | PR12_PROVENANCE_QUOTE_LOSS_2026-09-02.md §5 |

| **B6d-cms-escalation** | 🔴 **The producer died repeatedly and nothing escalated — and it is structural, not an oversight.** This pipeline is in **no producer registry**: its runs land in `ingestion_tracker`, which **no health view consumes**, while B6a's `run_log` → `v_pipeline_task_health` surface is gov-side. So the **45-day freshness bound was the ONLY thing that could ever catch it**, and it took two months. **A failing producer should be louder than a stale table** — the freshness monitor measures a *symptom*; nothing watched the *cause*. This is **B6a Class 21 one layer up**: B6a fixed *"a skipped step emits nothing"*; here **the step emits, into a table with no consumer.** Cheapest honest version: alert on any row reaching `abandoned`, and on `max(started_at) − max(started_at where run_status='success')` exceeding the feed's own cadence. | **B6d-cms-lock** | 🟡 **`acquire_ingestion_lock` can reclaim the pipeline's OWN rows.** `ingest_medicare_clinics` calls it with `force=force or force_refresh`, so any forced run force-reclaims every `started` row for the dataset — **including the tracker rows the same run opened seconds earlier** — and marks them `failed`. That is the origin of the *"Reclaimed after 0.0h stale"* rows. Root shape: the lock writes its own row into `ingestion_tracker` under the **same `dataset_id`**, so lock rows and pipeline run rows are indistinguishable to its own query. B6d-cms fixed only the **message** (it hardcoded `"stale"` regardless of reason — a self-contradicting *"0.0h stale"* that cost a diagnosis cycle). The semantics are untouched: it is lock behaviour, not the outage, and deserves its own change. | 🟡 | B6d-cms §7 |

Diagnose before changing anything. | 🔴 | Lock 4 |
| ~~**B4**~~ | ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-28 (diagnosis only) — and it was the thread that produced B5.** dia is ahead on depth because of **one feeder gov never had**: `sales_transactions_seller_exit`, **2,207 of dia's 2,757 historical facts**. It closes the SELLER's ownership interval when a sale is recorded — a historical ownership fact by construction. **The transferable unlock is B5.** ⚠️ **The question that cracked it was "where do the FACTS come from", not "why is dia better"** — one `group by ownership_source` answered in a single query what the funnel audit could not see at all. **When one domain outperforms another on a metric, group that metric by its provenance column before theorising.** | ✅ | audit §3c |

## P0s — ⏸️ SECRETS EXPOSED IN THE REPO — ROTATION DEFERRED BY DECISION (Scott, 2026-08-29)

> 👤 **DECISION, 2026-08-29: all credential rotation is deferred until a second user is added to
> LCC.** Rationale: single-user, still building, private repo — the exposure has no second party to
> reach. **This is a recorded risk acceptance with a trigger, not an oversight**, and the rows stay
> open so it cannot go quiet.
>
> 🔔 **The trigger is broader than "another user" — the PRIVATE REPO is what carries this risk.**
> Rotate BEFORE any of: a second LCC user · **making the repo public** · **sharing it with a
> contractor, vendor or Northmarq IT** · a lost/compromised laptop · LCC leaving "still building"
> for anything a client touches.
>
> ✅ **SEC4 STAYS ACTIVE.** It is a guard, not a rotation: deferring rotation is a decision about the
> keys already exposed; **SEC4 is what stops the next PA export adding more.** Without it, the
> rotation you eventually do is against a moving target.
>
> ⚠️ **When rotation happens, ORDER MATTERS: rotate → update Railway → `git rm --cached` +
> `.gitignore` → only then consider history.** `git rm --cached` alone leaves the value in history
> and in every clone. **Do not reach for `filter-branch`** — this repo nearly lost a 475 MB mailbox
> that way.

⚠️ **These are credential exposures, not hygiene. They outrank every other row in this file.**
**Rotation is what neutralises them — removing a file from tracking does NOT**, because the value
remains in git history and in any clone or fork.

| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 🚨 **SEC2** | **`wave0-config-values.txt` is TRACKED IN GIT at the repo root and contains `LCC_API_KEY` in PLAINTEXT.** Verified 2026-08-28: 858 bytes, `git ls-files` confirms tracked, **not in `.gitignore`**. Also carries `LCC_HOST=https://life-command-center-nine.vercel.app` (**the retired host that still answers and still holds a service key**, P194) plus Teams tenant/team/channel IDs. ⚠️ **This is a DIFFERENT and worse exposure than SEC1**, which records the key as *"pasted in plaintext during a curl diagnostic"* — a chat paste is transient; **a tracked file is in history, in every clone, and in every fork.** **Order: (1) ROTATE `LCC_API_KEY` and update the Railway env; (2) `git rm --cached` + `.gitignore`; (3) only then consider history.** ⚠️ **Do NOT reach for `filter-branch` casually** — this repo has a documented near-loss of a 475 MB mailbox doing exactly that (`CLAUDE.md`, destructive-op order). **Rotation alone makes the committed value worthless, which is the outcome that matters.** | 🔴 | `wave0-config-values.txt:6` |
| 🚨 **SEC3** | **TEN OF SEVENTEEN deployed Power Automate packages contain literal JWT-like values**, per `docs/os/POWER-AUTOMATE-DEPLOYED-CATALOG.md`. `docs/architecture/flows/sync-sf-activities-to-supabase.md` carries an unresolved **P0**: *"plaintext credential material detected in exported definition — rotate exposed Supabase keys immediately"*, with **`Credential rotation completed: TBD`**. ⚠️ **I1 covers only the `X-PA-Webhook-Secret`; the Supabase key rotation across those ten packages is a SEPARATE and larger item that was never filed.** Establish which keys, whether any are service-role, and rotate. | 🔴 | flows catalog |
| **SEC4** | **Every PA flow export committed to this repo is a credential-bearing artefact by default.** SEC2 and SEC3 are two instances of one pattern. **Add a pre-commit or CI check for JWT-shaped / `sb_secret_` / long-hex strings in `*.json` flow exports and `*.txt` config files** — the guard this repo has for conflict markers, applied to secrets. Without it the next export re-introduces the same exposure silently. | 🔴 | derived |

## P0d — 🏛️ DATA COHERENCE: make the connections assertable, across every database

**Scott's standing requirement, 2026-08-28:** *"eliminate these exact types of code and/or logical
gaps or misconnections… in all our databases (current two and planned others) so that all data
sources and ingestion is propelling the entire database forward, not just a bunch of different
component parts or subdatabases or tables."*

📍 **Contract:** [`docs/architecture/data-coherence-invariants.md`](../architecture/data-coherence-invariants.md)
— ten invariants (I1–I10), the new-database onboarding checklist, and the honest detector status:
**two of ten invariants have a standing detector.** Everything else was found by a human asking the
right question. **These rows turn the highest-yield ones into scheduled checks.**

⚠️ **Every defect behind this campaign was individually correct code.** Component tests, boot checks
and health views all passed. **These are properties of CONNECTIONS, which nothing currently asserts.**

| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **D1** | **I2 — the provenance producer-set diff, as a standing view.** The single highest-yield query in the system: group a fact store by its provenance column, split by domain; **a bucket present for one domain and absent for another IS the finding**, no hypothesis needed. It found B5 (gov never consumed its own sales table — 2,776 rows / 2,000 properties / **677 with no prior history at all**). **Run it over every shared fact store, not just ownership.** ⚠️ **A difference is not automatically a defect** — dia has no GSA lease inventory and that is legitimate. The view must let a difference be **explained and acknowledged**, or it becomes a badge of noise. | 🔴 | Class 20 · ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-29** — `scripts/d1-cross-db-provenance-diff.mjs` + planner `api/_shared/provenance-diff-planner.js` + ledger `scripts/d1-provenance-acknowledgements.json` + guard `test/d1-cross-db-provenance-diff.test.mjs` (18 tests, **19 mutations verified RED**). Writeup: [`D1`](../audits/D1_CROSS_DB_PROVENANCE_DIFF_2026-08-29.md). **Re-run MONTHLY** and on adding any source or domain DB. **First run: 69 differences over 23 two-sided stores — 58 legitimate, 5 unexplained, 6 unwired, NONE B5-sized.** ⚠️ **That is the real result: the two domains are substantially coherent, and the detector's value is now preventing the next divergence rather than closing a current one.** ⚠️ **THE BIGGER FINDING IS A PRECONDITION NOBODY STATED — 12 tables exist in both domains and record provenance in only ONE, so I2 cannot be evaluated on them at all**; the one that matters is **dia `ownership_history` (10,037 rows, no provenance column)** — the very store B5 was about (**D1g**). ⚠️ Five ways the query returns a confident wrong answer, all hit live and all now guarded: per-domain column NAMES (`properties` = `data_source`/`source`), a **dead** second column (gov `property_financials.source`, **0 of 98,510**), per-row suffixes, a provenance column holding a DATA VALUE at modest cardinality (`ingestion_tracker.source` = temp paths), and one producer wearing two labels. ⚠️ **NOT scheduled** — a job that has never run green once is the badge people merge past (**D1h**); the script's I/O path is **unexercised** because the sandbox holds no domain credentials. |

Size that before building. | 🔴 | Class 2 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |

Surfaced, deliberately not root-caused. | 🟡 | Class 20 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |

Also gov `recorded_owners` (17,242) and gov `available_listings` (3,131). ⚠️ **Not a one-liner**: adding a provenance column to a live store needs a writer audit and a backfill, and bundling that into an audit is how a repair becomes indistinguishable from the producer (P176). | 🔴 | I2 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |

The script's I/O path (`.env.local`, the `exec_sql` seam, the catalogue query) is **unexercised**: the sandbox holds no `GOV_/DIA_SUPABASE_*` credentials, so every D1 number was measured through the Supabase MCP seam instead. **First credentialed run is an operator step.** | 🟡 | Class 8 · Found by D1, 2026-08-29 |

Silent, no ledger, unrecoverable. **One instance is fixed; nothing has audited the others.** Enumerate every propagation trigger on all three DBs and check each is fill-forward, positive-controlled **both** directions. | 🔴 | I8 · gov CLAUDE.md §13a |

Start descriptive (a registry table populated from what B6 already swept) before making it enforcing. **Do not build this before D1/D2** — they are cheap and find real defects today. | 🔴 | I1 |

Enumerate fact stores lacking `created_at`; add it additively (never backfill a guess). | 🔴 | I9 |

a model `year1_rent` number is IGNORED whenever a quote is present (on the tenant AND each `rent_schedule` row), a date filled beside a vague precision is DROPPED, and an expiration is derived only from a stated commencement DAY plus a stated term — stamped `derived_from_term`, refusing every partial input. ⚠️ **Two judgement calls, stated:** an amount with NO stated basis resolves to `null` + `rent_basis_unresolved` rather than passing through as annual (*the same guess, in the other direction* — this is the one place EXT1 can lower coverage, and only where the number was unearned); and *the lease states no rent* keeps that flag **false**, because it is a different fact from *we cannot convert the rent it states* (P180). **Consumer contract unchanged** — the six graded keys keep their names and types, `rent_schedule` keeps `annual_rent`, and a bare legacy number or date string still resolves, so **no backfill**. ⚠️ **PREDICTED, not measured:** `year1_rent` self-rate → ~100%, `lease_expiration` → rises. ## P1c — Multi-party ownership: JV, fund and partnership structures (Scott, 2026-08-27)

**New requirement, raised while reviewing V8.** Scott: *"I think we connect JV LLC or SPEs like we
do recorded owner to LLC/SPE to true owner, but link to **multiple true owners** for each true owner
in the JV. Partnerships are going to be a good example of this down the road. We will have
investors that own assets outright, in JV and maybe in a fund like a DST or something similar."*

**This is a data-model change, not a lane fix**, and it is the first requirement in this arc that
the current model cannot express. Today the chain is single-valued: `recorded owner → SPE → ONE
true owner`. A joint venture has **two or more principals behind one SPE**, each of whom we
prospect independently.

| # | Item | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **J1** | **Design the multi-owner ownership edge** — one SPE resolving to **N** true owners, each with a role (JV partner, GP/LP, DST beneficiary, sole owner) and ideally a share. Must not break the existing single-owner path, which is nearly all of the data. | 🟢 👤 | `entity_relationships` may already carry the shape; **check before adding a table** |
| **J2** | **`Boyd Watterson JV UBP` is the live worked example** — Scott approved it into the Boyd family, and it is a JV, so today it resolves to Boyd alone and the second partner (`UBP`?) is invisible. **Use it as the design's test case.** | 🟢 | V8 row 1 |
| **J3** | **Leasehold vs fee is a REAL ownership split, not a weaker claim.** Scott: a `Lessee` SPE holds the **leasehold** (improvements) under a ground lease while another party holds the **fee** (dirt); the leasehold SPE is the landlord counterparty to the tenant. `Cr Sunflower Lessee LLC` is the live example. **The model should distinguish fee / leasehold / both**, or a ground-leased asset silently reads as one owner when it has two. | 🟢 | V8 row 3 |
| **J4** | **Downstream consequence — prospecting.** If an asset has two JV principals, both belong in the pursuit surfaces, and neither should be suppressed because the other is recorded. Check what `v_lcc_owner_contact_worklist` and the Tier 0 lane do with a multi-owner asset **before** J1 ships. | 🔴 | own item |

## P10 — Known, sized, unfixed data defects (carried from `CLAUDE.md`)

These are *measured* problems with a *known* shape. They are not vague.

| # | Item | Size |
|---|---|---|
| K1 | **`lcc_reconcile_property_owner` scores an ownership CHAIN as competing claims.** Recency decay floored at 0.25 means a thrice-sold building yields three near-equal candidates under the 0.55 gate. **876 assets have evidence and still read "Unresolved."** A strict-latest-purchase supersession tier would resolve **465**, correctly abstaining on the 360 that tie on date. **Adding another feeder does not fix this class — don't reach for one first.** |
| K2 | **The SAM tier ladder vs the real quota.** Tiers 0 (131) and 1 (514) run before tier 2 — and **tier 2 holds 9,153 valued owners topping out at $62.3M**. Sized for 600/day; the key actually delivers ~10/day, so the $62.3M owner is ~2 years out. Either interleave value across tiers or raise the SAM account tier. **Scott's call.** |
| K3 | **`v_field_provenance_unranked` returns 35 rows** for tables other than `entities.email/phone` — pre-existing writer/ladder drift. Should be 0. |
| K4 | **`v_owner_contact_enrich_queue`'s exclusion never expires.** All 316 `owner_contact_manual` tasks are `queued` and none has ever changed status, so the owners are permanently removed from automated processing — while **115 of them ($102.4M) already carry a genuine named active contact.** *Ask what event sets this state false, and whether anything ever fires it.* |
| K5 | **`v_lcc_weak_reach_worklist`** — 93 owners ($153.8M of annual rent) gated out of "reachable" by P161 because their only route is a weak `works_at` edge. They are the contact-acquisition engine's queue, not a defect. |
| K6 | **`v_lcc_portfolio_ownership_conflict`** — 12 ghost-vs-survivor portfolio conflicts, never auto-resolved by design (deleting the ghost would resolve toward the stale side). |
| K7 | **gov firm-term tail** — 625 sold-cap chart rows still lack a term; ~178 are STATE/municipal (no federal register can cover them; the state-lease producer has been **silent since 2026-06-23**), and 58 properties are OCR-pending. Surfaced in `v_gov_firm_term_backfill_queue` / `_reextract_queue`. |
| K8 | **gov `listing_verification_history`** records `prior_asking_price` but leaves `asking_price_at_check`/`price_delta` NULL on 5,636/5,637 rows. The reprice triggers made the chart writer-agnostic, but fixing the lvh writer would enrich the ledger. |
| K9 | **gov FRPP↔property matching pass.** The CoStar VA/SSA comp tail was never linked to its FRPP row (address-match ceiling ~29 of 533 distinct properties). FRPP holds `lease_expiration_date` on 19,756 leased assets — a geo/address matching pass is the real-volume unlock. |

### K13–K18 — the Capital-Markets chart defects (recovered 2026-08-27 from a root-level worklog)

⚠️ **These five were measured in detail on 2026-08-10/11 and never reached this backlog**, because
the P141 consolidation swept `docs/` and they lived in `CAPMARKETS_TAB_PACKET_WORKLOG.md` at the
repo **root**. Recovered while consolidating the root worklogs; source now archived at
`docs/history/worklogs/CAPMARKETS_TAB_PACKET_WORKLOG.md`. **Each carries its own live measurement —
re-measure before acting, but do not re-derive.**

| # | Item | Size |
|---|---|---|
| K13 | **`cm_gov_sold_cap_by_term_dot` uses the OLD term ladder, not `firm_term_years_at_sale`.** It prioritises `(gsa_leases.termination_date - sale_date)`, then `leases.firm_term_years - elapsed`, then the older `sales_transactions.firm_term_years` — the **opposite** of the dia R68/R66x pattern where the sale-frozen field is canonical. **1,368 cap-eligible sales bucket differently** under the two ladders; 2025–26 sales with 10+ years at sale plot as `<5` or `outside` because a stale GSA termination date wins first. Term data is NOT missing (3,211/3,211 populated). **Also the view's `cap_5to10` cohort is labelled `6-10`**, so 5–6yr sales sit in the middle bucket behind a wrong label. Fix: `COALESCE(firm_term_years_at_sale, lease fallback only when null)`, align buckets to 10+/6-10/<6/outside, keep the 2yr TTM + n>=5 gate, expose per-cohort n. **This is a stale view definition, not a data-ingestion failure.** |
| K14 | **`cm_gov_lease_termination_rate_m/_q` can select a CORRUPT partial snapshot as its active denominator.** `gsa_snapshots` 2019-02 holds **11 lease keys** against ~8,050 in adjacent months, so Feb-2019 plots `total_leases_active=11`. The view already builds a `clean_snap` set for departure endpoints but the active count still carries forward from raw `snap_agg`. Fix: carry forward the latest **clean** snapshot on/before the period using the same plausibility guard. |
| K15 | **Corrupt source snapshot months need re-ingest/repair** — `gsa_snapshots` 2019-02; `gsa_inventory_snapshot_lines` 2019-02, 2022-10, 2022-11. Separate from K14: fixing the view stops this chart hitting them, but every other consumer still can. |
| K16 | **`cm_gov_rent_price_psf_q` has no display policy and pre-1997 history is not reliable.** 1990–96 has `rent_psf` null throughout (`n_with_rent_ttm=0`) while `price_psf` rides 3–4 TTM observations (1994-12 ≈ $638/SF, 1995-12 ≈ $528/SF). First sustained period by a conservative gate (8 consecutive quarters, both series populated, both n>=4) is **1997-06-30**; density is materially better from 2003 and strong 2005+. No `rent_and_price_psf` row exists in the gov `cm_view_registry`, so nothing is cropped today. **Scott's call: 1997-06-30 for maximum history, or 2003-01-01 to prioritise sample density.** |
| K17 | **`cm_gov_market_turnover_m` — export-side crop applied in code, DB and export policy have not converged.** Pre-2012 rows are artifacts (2005-01 `active_count=1`, 2008-12 `=2`) against 79–180 TTM sales. A curated `resolveEffectiveDisplayFrom()` fallback pins gov market_turnover at 2012-01-01 in the exporter, **because the live gov `cm_view_registry` has no `display_from` column** (`42703`). Fix: migrate the registry columns or crop in the view, so source and export agree. |
| K19 | **gov seller-sentiment fix was never mirrored to dia.** `cm-excel-export.js` drops the `_8q` columns and relabels `10+ yr` → `6+ yr` for gov; the 2026-08-12 follow-up ("mirror the fix to dia + recent-tail coverage guard") is recorded as intent with no evidence it landed. Re-measure before acting. → `docs/history/worklogs/GOVERNMENT_SELLER_SENTIMENT_WORKLOG.md` |
| K20 | **dia 23654 Census-radius demographics write never completed.** Prompt 16's live-apply checklist items 1–2 were applied and re-verified; **item 3 (the Census-radius demographics write) is the remaining one.** `scripts/backfill-dia-location-trade-area-23654.mjs` passes syntax check and `.env.local` carries a `CENSUS_API_KEY`. Related to the un-re-measured "Census key" note carried in `CURRENT-STATE.md` §7. → `docs/history/worklogs/DOSSIER_LOCATION_TRADE_AREA_23654_WORKLOG.md` |
| K18 | **`cm_gov_core_cap_rate_dots` retains a lease-derived term fallback that nothing currently uses.** Audited 2026-08-11: all 1,143 plotted dots come from sale-frozen `firm_term_years_at_sale`; `lease_fallback_term_dots = 0`. **Acceptable for current report use** — but future unbackfilled sales could enter through the fallback. Fix if stricter provenance is wanted: expose a term-source flag, or require `firm_term_years_at_sale IS NOT NULL` in the core-dot wrapper. |

## P15 — dia ownership lane: coverage, buyer→company linkage, pattern precision (measured 2026-08-29)

Filed from `docs/audits/DIA_OWNERSHIP_LANE_COVERAGE_2026-08-29.md`, written after the
Deals ▸ Ownership statement-timeout fix made the lane render. **Everything below is measured,
nothing is built.** The lane shows 16 canonicals / 61 variants / 500 properties; the audit
explains what that is a fraction *of*.

⚠️ **The one thing to carry forward if you read nothing else:**
`v_recorded_owner_canonical_clusters` is a readout of the **38-row hand-curated
`owner_canonical_patterns` table**, not a detector. `dia_canonicalize_owner_name` returns the
name unchanged when no pattern matches, and there are **ZERO byte-identical duplicate
`recorded_owners.name` values** — so **72 of 7,255 owners (1.0%) can ever appear**, and 100%
of what shows is the patterns showing themselves. The panel copy has been corrected to say so.

| # | Item | State | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OWN1** | 👤 **Two `owner_canonical_patterns` regexes over-capture, and one wrong link is ALREADY WRITTEN.** `^healthcare\s+realty(\s+trust)?` has no end anchor, so **`HealthCare Realty Solutions`** canonicalizes to `Healthcare Realty Trust` — a different company, and tellingly the one *unlinked* member of that cluster. `^(sumitomo\s+bank\s+leasing\|smbc\s+leasing\|sumitomo\s+mitsui)` matches any `sumitomo mitsui …`, so **`Sumitomo Mitsui Trust Bank`** (Sumitomo Mitsui **Trust** Holdings — a different corporate group from SMBC/SMFG) is folded into SMBC **and already carries SMBC's `true_owner_id`**. Same pattern also swallows **`SMBC Leasing & Finance Inc, Stanley F & Jane M Banach`**, two named individuals (the P158a `&` hazard, reaching a write path). **Blast radius today is ZERO — all three hold 0 properties**, which is exactly why to fix it before they acquire one. Two-line change; **surfaced not applied** because it is a judgement about company identity. | 🔴 👤 | measured on named rows 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN2** | **The lane's true-owner hygiene is a 10-row job.** 6 variants carry no `true_owner_id` (SMBC ×3, Agree Realty, Healthcare Realty, Capital Square 1031); 4 canonicals resolve to two `true_owners` each (SMBC/SMFG, AEI Capital / AEI Capital Corporation — **AEI is actually split three ways**, a third `Aei Capital Corp` holds 8 properties — Healthcare Realty Trust / …Incorporated, Societe Generale / SG Mortgage Finance Corp). Small and decidable; it also clears two `v_lcc_merge_candidates` rows. **Do OWN1 first** — one of the 6 unlinked rows is the Healthcare false positive and must not be linked. | 🔴 | measured 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN3** | **2 of 10 split `true_owners` have NO LCC entity** (`SMFG`, `AEI Capital Corporation`) — no `external_identities(dia/true_owner)` row, so they are invisible to every LCC owner surface. The other 8 resolve to live entities, none tombstoned. Fleet-wide the linkage is broadly in place: **6,568** dia true_owner identities against **1,128** dia true_owners that actually hold a property. | 🔴 | measured 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN4** | 👤 **THE ONLY LEVER THAT MOVES COVERAGE: the owner slot is occupied by the TENANT.** Of the 500 lane properties, **395 (79%)** have a `true_owner` that is a **flagged** operator (DaVita / Fresenius / American Renal — `is_operator_not_owner` is set, so the system already knows), 5 a placeholder, 100 a plausible landlord. Realty Income holds 72 properties reading *American Renal*; MassMutual 47 reading *Fresenius*; Elliott Bay Capital 25, all *DaVita*. Beyond the lane, **4,028 more properties have no recorded owner at all and carry an operator as true_owner.** This is the documented **P113** trap at scale. `dia_unify_canonical_true_owners` **cannot** do it — its plan requires `is_operator_not_owner IS NOT TRUE`, and that refusal is correct. **A supersession pass is a doctrine decision (overwriting a populated, flagged owner slot), not plumbing — size it before building.** Reuse the existing flag; never write a second name-based operator test. | 🔴 👤 | measured 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN5** | ⚠️ **Do NOT widen the grouping key to chase group count — the trade was measured.** Case+punct-insensitive grouping finds **300** groups / 631 variants / **463 properties**; `dia_norm_owner_name` finds **385** / 826 / **652** — against today's 16 / 61 / **500**. ~20× the GROUPS for roughly the SAME properties, because the 38 patterns already capture the big consolidators and the tail is two-row variants holding 1–2 properties. It is a hygiene win, not a coverage win. And `dia_norm_owner_name` is a legal-form stripper: sanctioned for **grouping-for-review**, **banned for identity** (same class as `dup-pair-planner.ownerCore` / `lcc_normalize_entity_name` / `lcc_owner_strict_core`). If built: review queue with a human verdict, never an auto-merge. | 🔴 | measured 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN6** | **`dia_unify_canonical_true_owners` has no caller — and is nearly drained.** No cron invokes it (crons 38/42/46/47/53 cover other owner work); it is a manual one-shot, which is the Class 8 "one-shot repair of a recurring producer" shape. Low urgency: today's dry run is **0 created / 14 recorded_owners / 7 properties**. Decide whether to schedule it or retire it — but note it can never touch OWN4's 395, by design. | 🔴 | measured 2026-08-29 |
| **OWN7** | **`dia_canonicalize_owner_name` staleness now has a standing detector — keep it at 0.** The timeout fix materialized the canonical onto `recorded_owners.canonical_name` behind a single-writer trigger. The stored value tracks `name`, **not** `owner_canonical_patterns` — so **any edit to that table (OWN1 included) must be followed by `select * from dia_recanonicalize_recorded_owners(false);`**. `v_dia_canonical_name_drift` must read 0 rows and is positive-controlled. A non-zero row means a pattern edit was not re-applied, or a writer escaped the trigger. | 🟢 | shipped 2026-08-29 |

## P10a — Cross-lane property identity and address resolution (designed, not built)

The restricted ASC sample exposed a platform class: property identity cannot be maintained as an expanding
set of lane-local string replacements. Canonical contract:
[`property-identity-and-address-resolution.md`](../architecture/property-identity-and-address-resolution.md).
All rows are aggregate-only and preserve the current fail-closed research boundary.

| # | Item | State | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| PI1 | **Inventory and version the existing ASC identity rules.** Name each transformation, evidence burden, reason code, collision guard, second-review requirement, and owner. Preserve historical frozen-token compatibility as an explicit rule, not an implicit re-normalization. | 🟢 | property identity contract §5–7; restricted ASC observations 2026-08-28 |
| PI2 | **Build a de-identified golden mismatch corpus with adversarial negative controls.** Cover suites/floors, parent buildings, same-address campuses, ranges, directionals, suffixes, municipality aliases, compound streets, postal variants, historical tokens, and confusable facility names. No private rows or licensed payloads in Git. | 🟢 | property identity contract §7 |
| PI3 | **Extract a lane-neutral pure matcher and structured decision object.** The component must be deterministic, versioned, write-free, and fail closed; existing ASC outcomes replay unchanged except separately approved corrections. | 🟢 | property identity contract §4–6 |
| PI4 | **Add sidebar identity diagnostics and explicit candidate refresh/advance state.** Show the active frozen candidate, component verdicts, corroboration, rule version, second-review flag, failure class, and one safe next action. Eliminate stale-candidate ambiguity without retargeting on page navigation. | 🟢 | property identity contract §8 |
| PI5 | **Run read-only shadow evaluation across approved current and future lanes.** Report changed decisions, collisions, and review load before any lane adopts a shared rule. No canonical writes or scope expansion. | 🟢 | property identity contract §7, §11 |
| PI6 | **Design a governed property-alias ledger.** Every alias needs citations, scope, authorization, timestamps, reason, review/expiry behavior, replay, and rollback. This requires a separate schema/security decision before migration or use. | 🔴 👤 | property identity contract §5, §11 |
| PI7 | **Emit aggregate identity-quality metrics.** Measure match modes, missingness, disagreements, collisions, second-review agreement, stale-state errors, technical failures, and research time without exposing row-level or licensed data. | 🟢 | property identity contract §10 |
| PI8 | **Optional on-box discrepancy-note assistant.** After PI3 emits structured decisions, an on-box model may suggest reason codes or draft reviewer notes. It is advisory only and may never match, alias, approve, or write. | ⚪ | property identity contract §9 |

**Gate:** complete and aggregate-review the frozen 50-property ASC sample before Phase B/PI2–PI3 begins.
Activation in any lane remains a separate decision.

## P12 — Explicitly excluded (kept so they are not re-proposed)

| Item | Why |
|---|---|
| **Work IQ Mail / Teams connectors** | Excluded by decision — email and comms stay on the LCC path. |
| **A PATCH projector for the 2,809 Outlook contacts** | Measured: ~144 field-values, most of which came *from* Outlook. Would run green and move nothing. |
| **Any CAPTCHA-solving service for SOS egress** | Standing prohibition. The unlock is client fidelity (cookie jar + browser-grade TLS), not defeating a challenge. |
| **Scraping LinkedIn** | ToS. Use the user's own connections export, company team pages, SEC filings, or a licensed API. |
| **Name-keyed web/LinkedIn enrichment** | Confidently moves people to the wrong firm (a 2026-08-26 search returned a *different* Andrew Pulliam). Key on email domain + employer corroboration, never a name match. |
| **`Deal_Participants__c` / SF OpportunityContactRole as the deal-party roster** | Verified dead end for Team Briggs — OCR is empty on all 592 backbone deals; `Deal_Participants__c.Deal` is `Fannie_Mae_Deal__c`. The correspondence-driven matcher is the roster. |

## P13 — Decision forks (Scott's call; do not build past them)

1. **Entity reconciliation** — 232 deals in multi-asset cities are parked as flagged entities. Auto-merge
   on best-match with a review queue, require manual confirmation for all 232, or treat the flagged
   entity as canonical and merge lazily as signal arrives?
2. **Team mailbox intake (Kelly / Sarah / Nate)** — the single biggest cadence-accuracy lever (LCC sees
   only Scott's mailbox; every outlook row is `SYSTEM_ACTOR`). Which mailboxes, what auth model, how to
   attribute per broker. **Deferred by Scott.** Phase 1 (attribution) is buildable independently.
3. **Per-broker delivery (owner-scoped digests)** — live in the engine, specced as a PA flow,
   **PARKED by Scott** until build-out is done and errors are triaged.
4. **Cadence tiering: computed vs manual** (the D12 red line).
5. **The clean-assist rank scales** (N1) — re-ranking `dia_xref` also re-orders the human-facing
   Decision Center lane.
6. **SAM account tier vs interleaving value across tiers** (K2).

---

### How to keep this file honest

- When a row ships, **move it to `CURRENT-STATE.md` §2 and delete it here** — do not leave a ✅ row,
  that is how a backlog rots into a changelog.
- When a row is genuinely retired, **move it to P12 with the reason**, never delete it.
- When measurement refutes a row's premise (it happens often here), **rewrite the row with the
  measurement** rather than silently dropping it — the correction is usually the more valuable artifact.
